## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 per robot outside the target region (BLUE-walled strip with BLACK floor)
- Target: get all robots to the BLACK floor strip near BLUE walls
- Key perceptions: floor color (BLACK = target strip or wrong strip), wall colors (BLUE vs GREEN)
- The floor is BLACK in BOTH strips, GRAY in between
- We need robots to go toward BLUE walls and stay there

**Key Insight:**
- BLACK floor = either target or non-target strip
- GRAY floor = middle area
- BLUE walls = target side (color 3 in ARGoS? Let me think... colors: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)
- GoToColor with clr=3 (blue) should navigate toward blue walls
- GoAwayColor with clr=2 (green) should move away from green walls

**Champion Analysis:**
The champion (17865.69) uses ZigZag + Exploration + GoAwayColor(yellow/4) + Stop. The GoAwayColor(4=yellow) seems odd for this mission. The mission says BLUE walls are the target. Let me reconsider - maybe the color IDs map differently.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Exploit champion: tweak parameters slightly
3. Explore: Use GoToColor(blue=3) as primary behavior, or GoAwayColor(green=2) to flee green walls
4. Try simple 2-state machines: explore until BLACK floor detected, then stop/stay

**FSM Designs:**

1. **Champion** (copy exact)
2. **Exploit champion** - adjust probabilities slightly
3. **GoToColor blue** - move toward blue walls, stop when many neighbors present
4. **GoAwayColor green** - flee green walls
5. **2-state: explore + GoToColor blue** with floor transitions
6. **3-state: explore → GoToColor blue → stop**
7. **Simple GoToColor blue single state**
8. Various other explorations

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key findings from Round 2:**
- The champion (3943.22) uses GoToColor(blue=3) as the main behavior, stopping when on BLACK floor or when many neighbors present, and also avoiding green (clr=2). This is a massive improvement from 17865 to 3943!
- The structure: State 0 = GoToColor(blue), transitions back to itself on BLACK floor (p=0.9), on many neighbors (≥6, steep), or seeing green walls (p=0.4); State 1 = Stop, transitions back to State 0 on GRAY floor (p=0.9) or few neighbors.
- The key insight: GoToColor(blue=3) navigates toward blue walls. When robots reach BLACK floor (target strip), they stop. If they drift to gray, they resume seeking blue.

**Wait - the transition `--n0x0 0` points back to state 0 itself?** No, that's the destination index among OTHER states. With 2 states, leaving state 0, the only other state is state 1, so D=0 means state 1. Let me re-read...

Actually with 2 states, leaving state 0, D=0 targets state 1 (the only other state). So all transitions from state 0 go to state 1. And from state 1, D=0 goes back to state 0. This makes sense!

So:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) → go to state 1 (Stop) when BLACK floor (p=0.9), OR many neighbors (≥6), OR see green walls
- State 1: Stop → go to state 0 (GoToColor blue) when GRAY floor (p=0.9) or few neighbors

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim
2. Exploit: tweak probabilities/thresholds of champion
3. Explore: try 3-state versions, different color combinations, adding GoAwayColor(green)

**LED color fix:** Only 0, 4, 5, 6 are valid. I'll use 0 (off) for all.

## Reasoning

**Key findings:**
- Champion (3281.73): GoToColor(blue=3) → on BLACK floor or many neighbors → Attraction(gain=2) → on GRAY floor or few neighbors → back to GoToColor
- The Attraction behavior in state 1 (instead of Stop) is the key improvement! Robots aggregate together once they reach the blue strip.
- The 2-state structure works very well: GoToColor(blue) + Attraction

**Pattern analysis:**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue), transitions to state 1 on: BLACK floor (p=0.9), many neighbors (≥6, steep w=10), seeing green walls (p=0.4)
- State 1: Attraction(gain=2), transitions back to state 0 on: GRAY floor (p=0.9), few neighbors (<4, w=8)

**Exploitation strategy:**
- Vary attraction gain (1.0-5.0)
- Vary neighbor thresholds and steepness
- Vary floor detection probabilities
- Try adding a 3rd condition or state
- Try FlockCenter instead of Attraction

**Exploration:**
- Try 3-state: GoToColor → Attraction → Stop (deeper aggregation)
- Try different neighbor thresholds for stopping
- Try GoAwayColor(green) as an intermediate step

**LED colors:** Only 0, 4, 5, 6 allowed - use 0 throughout.

## Reasoning

**Analysis:**
- Champion (2885.90): GoToColor(blue=3) with transitions on BLACK floor (p=0.95), many neighbors (≥5, w=12), seeing green (p=0.35) → Attraction(gain=2), transitions back on GRAY floor (p=0.85) or few neighbors (<5, w=10)
- Key insight: return condition uses p1x0=0.85 (not 0.9) and few_neighbors threshold=5, w=10 - this is better than the 0.9/4/8 versions
- FlockCenter(30) also performed well (3028), competitive with Stop
- att=4 also competitive (3017)
- att=5 was worse, 4th transition (seeing blue) was worse

**Exploitation focus:**
- Vary att gain: 2, 3, 4 (sweet spot seems 2-4)
- Vary return-gray prob: 0.8, 0.85, 0.9
- Vary few-neighbors threshold: 4, 5, 6 with steepness 8, 10, 12
- Vary black floor prob: 0.93, 0.95, 0.97
- Vary neighbor-above threshold: 4, 5, 6 with w=12, 15
- Combine FlockCenter with champion's other params

**Exploration:**
- Try att=2 but with different return condition mixing gray+neighbor
- Try adding a 3rd transition to state 1 (e.g., seeing blue walls → return to seek)
- Try different green avoidance prob: 0.25, 0.3, 0.35

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key finding:** FlockCenter(fc=30) in state 1 beats Attraction! The champion uses FlockCenter instead of Stop or Attraction. This makes robots move toward the center of their group once they reach the blue strip, keeping them together in the target area.

**Champion structure:**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3), → state 1 on BLACK floor (p=0.95), many neighbors (≥5, w=12), or seeing green walls (p=0.35)
- State 1: FlockCenter(fc=30), → state 0 on GRAY floor (p=0.85) or few neighbors (<5, w=10)

**Exploitation focus:**
- Vary fc gain: 20, 25, 30, 40, 50 (sweet spot around 30)
- Vary few-neighbors threshold: 4, 5, 6
- Vary steepness w: 8, 10, 12
- Vary gray return prob: 0.8, 0.85, 0.9
- Vary neighbor-above threshold: 4, 5, 6 with w=10, 12, 15
- Vary green avoidance prob: 0.35 seems good, try 0.3 slightly worse so stay near 0.35-0.4

**Exploration:**
- Try fc=50, 60, 80 (higher flocking)
- Try adding a 3rd transition to state 1
- Try 3-state with GoToColor → FlockCenter → Stop
- Try different neighbor counts

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key findings from Round 7:**
- Champion confirmed: GoToColor(blue) + FlockCenter(fc=30) with specific parameters
- w=12 and w=15 both give same score (2185.47) - steepness doesn't matter much in this range
- w=12 for few-neighbors return also good (2186.49)
- fc=50 worse than 30 (2239), fc=20 much worse (2634)
- green_avoidance p=0.4 is worse than 0.35
- few-neighbors threshold=4 (w=10) is much worse (3225) - don't go below threshold 5
- few-neighbors threshold=6 (w=10) was ok (2265)

**Fine-tuning focus:**
- fc gain: try 25, 30, 35 (sweet spot near 30)
- few-neighbors: threshold 5, steepness 10-12 is the sweet spot
- gray return prob: 0.85 is good, try 0.8, 0.87, 0.9
- neighbor-above threshold: 5 with w=12-15 is good, try 4, 6
- black floor prob: 0.95 seems right
- green avoidance: 0.35 is best, avoid 0.4

**Exploration:**
- Try adding a 3rd state (Stop or Repulsion) for robots that overshoot
- Try neighbor condition in state 1 with different steepness
- Try fc=30 but with w=20 for the return condition

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key findings from Round 8:**
- Champion (2111.57): same topology, but return-to-gray prob changed to 0.9 (was 0.85). Higher p=0.9 for gray return is better!
- p=0.8 also good (2176), p=0.85 less good (2351-2586)
- fc=25 ok (2312), fc=35 worse (2676), fc=20 was worst before
- w=20 for few-neighbors return is bad (2586)
- 3-state versions continue to underperform badly
- The sweet spot: p_gray_return=0.9, fc=30, few_neighbors threshold=5 w=10

**Fine-tuning focus for Round 9:**
- gray return prob: try 0.88, 0.9, 0.92, 0.95
- fc: try 28, 30, 32 (very tight around 30)
- few-neighbors: threshold 5 w=10 seems good, try w=8, 12
- neighbor-above threshold: 5 w=12 is fixed, try w=10, 14
- black floor prob: 0.95 seems right
- green avoidance: 0.35 is best

**What to avoid:** fc=35+, w=20 for return, 3-state versions, p=0.8 or below for gray return

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 18 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.66 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
